## statement
Finite multiplicative phase covers and conditional full-certificate amplification for actual primitive seeds.

All vector spaces and inner products are finite-dimensional real Euclidean ones. Fix L>0. For every nonnegative integer n and every nonnegative schedule v=(v_0,...,v_(n-1)), let W_n(v) be the supremum of
    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)
over all convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient, all minimizers x_star of f, and all x_0!=x_star, where x_(i+1)=x_i-(v_i/L)grad f(x_i) for 0<=i<n. The supremum ranges over every finite dimension. The empty schedule is included. Write R_n(v) for the same supremum without the factor 2, so W_n=2R_n.

For nonnegative real x,y define
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.
Define the original ALL-split benchmark by C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),
where every split k is an integer. Put U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, lambda=1+sqrt(2), and rho=log_2(lambda). For every positive integer b, let Z_b be the finite positive limit lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1), whose existence is part of predecessor 04219554e41d9750. All logarithms denoted log are natural logarithms.

Fix an integer r>=1 and an ACTUAL nonnegative seed word h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)). Put s=sum_(i=0)^(r-1)h_i, A=1+s, and m=r+1. Suppose there is an entire real (r+1)-by-(r+1) matrix Lambda satisfying Lambda_ii=0 and Lambda_ij>=0 for EVERY ordered distinct i,j in {0,...,r}. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1. Define T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i, and set
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)),
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.
Here diag of a column is the diagonal matrix with that column; a superscript T means transpose. Require the EXACT flow equation L_flow^T e=omega and positive semidefiniteness of M_prim on the ENTIRE R^(r+1). Thus all r(r+1) ordered signs and all r+1 formal gradients are retained, including gradients at zero physical steps, with no coordinate deletion or imposed gradient equality. No symmetry, support, rank, zero-remainder, product-balance, or h_i>=1 condition is imposed. Nonzero and singular positive semidefinite remainders are allowed.

For each integer n>=0 choose any fully expanded maximizing tree of the original B_n recurrence, expanding every primitive C child by the original C recurrence. It has n internal nodes, n primitive empty leaves, and one dominant empty leaf. Replace each primitive empty leaf by the ACTUAL word h, leave the dominant empty leaf EMPTY with mass 1, and keep all internal nodes. Recompute every physical bridge and concatenate (left,bridge,right): a primitive join with actual child capacities A_left=1+sum(left) and A_right=1+sum(right) uses 1+d_pp, where d_pp>0 uniquely solves d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left A_right; a dominant join with primitive left capacity A_left and right dominant mass D_right=1+2sum(right) uses 1+A_left/zeta, where zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2. Denote the resulting physical word by Hsub_n and its actual mass by Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n. Ties may be fixed deterministically by the least maximizing split at every node.

The following conclusions hold jointly.

(1) The physical substitution has length(Hsub_n)=mn and the full-function equality
    W_(mn)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
    (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2<=Dsub_n<=A B_n.
The equality combines an objective upper certificate valid for the complete function class with a matching one-dimensional Huber example. For positive integers b<=c one has Z_b<=Z_c; for every integer ell>=0, Z_(2^ell b)=lambda^ell Z_b. For b>=1 and j>=0 define
    L_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1),
    U_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2,
    E(b,j)=8sqrt(2)b^(rho-1)2^(-j)+(3/25)(b+1)^(rho/2)lambda^(-j/2)+(9/10000)lambda^(-j).
Then L_pc(b,j)<=Z_b<=U_pc(b,j) and 0<=U_pc(b,j)-L_pc(b,j)<=E(b,j). For every integer d_shift>=0 with b*2^j-1-d_shift>=0,
    0<=Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d_shift)
      <=E(b,j)+8sqrt(2)d_shift b^(rho-1)2^(-j).

(2) General anchored finite cover. Choose positive integers b_pc0,d,K, an integer ell>=0, and a positive real C_pc with Z_d/Z_(b_pc0)<=C_pc. Suppose
    m^K b_pc0<=2^ell d,
    A^K>P_pc, where P_pc=lambda^ell C_pc.
For 0<=i<=K put b_pc_i=b_pc0 m^i. The exact finite product satisfies
    1<=product_(i=0)^(K-1) Z_(m b_pc_i)/Z_(b_pc_i)
      =Z_(m^K b_pc0)/Z_(b_pc0)<=P_pc.
In particular P_pc>=1. Choose any real certified capacity floor a and positive gamma with
    1<a<=A, a^K>P_pc, K gamma<=1-P_pc/a^K.
These strict cover assumptions admit such choices with a and gamma rational. The symbol a in this theorem denotes this local capacity floor. For 0<=i<K define q_pc_i(a)=Z_(m b_pc_i)/(a Z_(b_pc_i)). Some fixed i_star in this finite list satisfies
    Z_(m b_pc_i_star)/(A Z_(b_pc_i_star))<=q_pc_i_star(a)<=1-gamma.

(3) Exact physical rays, uniform limits, and certified selection. For 0<=i<K and integers j>=0 put
    n_(i,j)=b_pc_i 2^j-1,
    N_(i,j)=m n_(i,j)=m b_pc_i 2^j-m,
    H_(i,j)=Hsub_(n_(i,j)).
The true physical offset is N_(i,j)=m b_pc_i 2^j-1-(m-1). One has
    0<=A Z_(b_pc_i)-lambda^(-j)Dsub_(n_(i,j))
      <=A E(b_pc_i,j)+2(sqrt(A)-1)sqrt(A Z_(b_pc_i))lambda^(-j/2),
    lim_(j->infinity)B_(N_(i,j)) W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
      =Z_(m b_pc_i)/(A Z_(b_pc_i)).
The convergence is uniform over the finite phase list and over all maximizing-tree ties.

At stage t>=0, define the certified ratio upper bound
    F_pc_i(t)=U_pc(m b_pc_i,t)/(a L_pc(b_pc_i,t)).
The rule that takes the first integer stage t at which some F_pc_i(t)<1-gamma/2 and then freezes the smallest successful index i terminates after finitely many stages. It does not require finding a minimizing phase. When a and gamma are supplied as certified rational inputs, there is an entirely rational outward-enclosure version: at each integer stage t>=1, enclose each L_pc(b_pc_i,t) and U_pc(m b_pc_i,t) with absolute error at most 2^(-t), using a positive lower endpoint for L_pc and an upper endpoint for U_pc, and accept only upon the resulting strict rational test. This version also terminates and its chosen index has the same conclusion. For an arbitrary real seed, existence of rational a,gamma is asserted, but their effective certification must be part of the supplied input; no capacity oracle is presumed.

Set b_pc_max=b_pc_(K-1) and
    J=ceil(log(60 b_pc_max/gamma)/log(3/2)).
Equivalently J is the least nonnegative integer satisfying (3/2)^J>=60 b_pc_max/gamma. For the one fixed index i selected by either certified rule, EVERY integer j>=J satisfies
    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-gamma/4) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.
This includes the equality case in the integer test defining J. The horizons N_(i,j) form a strictly increasing unbounded integer sequence. Search stage t and output depth j are distinct; no bound t<=J or computational efficiency is asserted. The phase selection and all schedules depend only on the seed and benchmark, before any objective or trajectory is observed.

(4) Unanchored equivalence. With b_pc0=d=1 and C_pc=1, the existence of integers K>=1, ell>=0 with m^K<=2^ell and A^K>lambda^ell is equivalent to A>m^rho. Hence A>(r+1)^rho is a sufficient seed-capacity interface for the conclusion in (3). For a general certified comparison Z_d/Z_(b_pc0)<=C_pc, the anchored sufficient capacity is A>(lambda^ell C_pc)^(1/K) whenever m^K b_pc0<=2^ell d. At K=1, ell=0, b_pc0=3 and d=3m, the accepted choices C_pc=Gamma5_c107=7977607/806000 for r=5 and C_pc=Gamma6_c107=387153999/32240000 for r=6 recover the sufficient capacity conditions of predecessor 4c92d3fc4eb6153e; that predecessor's specialized depth bounds retain their own proved scope.

(5) Three exact rational corollaries, each conditional on the entire seed hypothesis stated above:

If r=5 and A>=49/5, use m=6, a=49/5, K=17, ell=44, b_pc0=d=C_pc=1 and gamma=1/10000. There is one certified selected index i in {0,...,16}, with b_pc_i=6^i and N_(i,j)=6^(i+1)2^j-6, such that for EVERY
    j>=J5=ceil(log(600000*6^16)/log(3/2)),
    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/40000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.

If r=6 and A>=12, use m=7, a=12, K=11, ell=31, b_pc0=d=C_pc=1 and gamma=1/20000. There is one certified selected index i in {0,...,10}, with b_pc_i=7^i and N_(i,j)=7^(i+1)2^j-7, such that for EVERY
    j>=J6=ceil(log(1200000*7^10)/log(3/2)),
    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/80000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.

If r=5 and A>=197/20, use m=6, a=197/20, K=9, ell=21, b_pc0=3, d=15, C_pc=19299/2500 and gamma=1/2000. The accepted strict comparison Z_15/Z_3<C_pc supplies the anchor. There is one certified selected index i in {0,...,8}, with b_pc_i=3*6^i and N_(i,j)=18*6^i*2^j-6, such that for EVERY
    j>=J5a=ceil(log(360000*6^8)/log(3/2)),
    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/8000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.
At this same K=9, the least unanchored exponent with 6^9<=2^ell is ell=24, whereas lambda^21 C_pc<lambda^24. This compares these finite product bounds only, without claiming phase optimality. The sufficient floors 49/5, 12, and 197/20 improve the respective earlier convenient floors 99/10, 1201/100, and 99/10 by 1/10, 1/100, and 1/20.

This theorem is conditional. It supplies no actual seed or multiplier array and does not settle the unrestricted exact-profile problem. It asserts no arbitrary-template inflation, exponent-based finite capacity ceiling, real-base phase interpolation, global phase optimum, all-horizon affirmative result, or anytime schedule. The appearance of A^K is only a product-of-ratios argument, not construction of a new seed with that capacity.

## proof
# Complete joint proof: finite phase covers for actual full-seed insertion

The only mathematical predecessors invoked are accepted facts
04219554e41d9750 and 4c92d3fc4eb6153e. The independent audit is not a
mathematical premise. All newly proved parts below are submitted jointly.
The symbol b_pc_i denotes an integer phase base, not a checkpoint step.
The symbol a in Sections 4--9 is a local certified capacity floor.
In Section 1, f_i=f(x_i) and g_i=grad f(x_i) after normalization L=1.
The symbol log denotes the natural logarithm and log_2 the base-two logarithm.
Every stage, phase and depth index is an integer in its stated range.

## 1. Original model and complete seed hypothesis

For a nonnegative integer n and a nonnegative schedule
h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), define W_n(h) to be the supremum of

    2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L ||x_0-x_star||^2)

over every finite-dimensional Euclidean space, every convex differentiable
function f with L-Lipschitz gradient and at least one minimizer x_star,
and every x_0!=x_star, under

    x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L) grad f(x_i).

Here L>0 is fixed but arbitrary. This is the original W=2R normalization;
the empty schedule is included.

For nonnegative a,d set

    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,
    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.

Retain all original integer splits:

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)),      n>=1.

Write U_n^ZJ=1/B_n, lambda=1+sqrt(2), and rho=log_2(lambda).

Fix an integer r>=1. A seed in this theorem means an ACTUAL word
h=(h_0,...,h_(r-1)) with every h_i>=0, sum s, capacity A=1+s,
and m=r+1, together with a full real (r+1)-by-(r+1) array Lambda.
For EVERY ordered distinct pair i,j in {0,...,r}, require

    Lambda_ij>=0,     Lambda_ii=0.

Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1, and define

    T_ii=1,  T_it=h_t for t<i,  T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

The full original-coordinate certificate requirements are

    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0 on R^(r+1).       (1)

Transpose and positive semidefiniteness have their ordinary real-matrix
meanings. There are exactly r(r+1) off-diagonal multipliers. Every formal
gradient g_0,...,g_r remains, even when physical steps vanish. No gradient
equality or coordinate deletion is allowed at a zero step. There is no
assumption h_i>=1, product balance, zero remainder, rank, support, or
symmetry. These are precisely the general-length sufficient hypotheses
in accepted `04219554e41d9750`, proof Section 10.

For clarity about their scope, that accepted result normalizes L=1 and
uses x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i) h_t g_t and

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
        +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

Its exact identity is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j) Lambda_ij Q_ij
       =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

Actual smooth convex trajectories have Q_ij<=0. Thus (1) implies the
primitive inequality on the full function class. Here Gram is the matrix
of pairwise inner products, and the matrix pairing is trace(M^T Gram).
This accepted implication is only sufficiency; no attainment or equivalence
of certificate classes is assumed.

## 2. Accepted ray and physical-substitution inputs

The following are precise statements used from `04219554e41d9750`.
For every positive integer b, the limit

    Z_b=lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1)

exists and is finite and positive. For every integer j>=0 put

    L_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),
    U_pc(b,j)=lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2,

    E(b,j)=8sqrt(2)b^(rho-1)2^(-j)
           +(3/25)(b+1)^(rho/2)lambda^(-j/2)
           +(9/10000)lambda^(-j).

The accepted enclosures and their proof give

    L_pc(b,j)<=Z_b<=U_pc(b,j),
    0<=U_pc(b,j)-L_pc(b,j)<=E(b,j).                         (2)

Indeed the latter is exactly the bound obtained by subtracting the two
endpoints in accepted Section 9: use the accepted adjacent bound for
B_(b*2^j)-B_(b*2^j-1) and B_n<=4C_n<=4(n+1)^rho. In particular both
endpoints tend to Z_b. For every fixed integer d>=0 and defined index,

    0<=Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d)
       <=E(b,j)+8sqrt(2)d b^(rho-1)2^(-j).                  (3)

The same accepted proof gives

    1<rho<4/3,
    B_n>=C_n,
    C_(b*2^j-1)=lambda^j C_(b-1),
    C_(b-1)>=1.                                           (4)

For every nonnegative n, take any fully expanded maximizing tree of the
ORIGINAL B_n recurrence, expanding every primitive child using the
ORIGINAL C recurrence. Such a tree has n internal nodes, n primitive
empty leaves, and one terminal dominant empty leaf. In particular,
there is no restriction on a maximizing dominant split.

Replace each primitive empty leaf by the actual seed of Section 1.
Retain all internal nodes and keep the dominant empty leaf empty with
mass 1. Recompute all physical bridges:

* At a primitive join, for actual child capacities A_left,A_right, use
  bridge 1+d_pp, where d_pp>0 solves
  d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left A_right.
* At a dominant join, for primitive left capacity A_left and actual
  dominant right mass D_right=1+2sum(right), use bridge
  1+A_left/zeta, where zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2.

Concatenate (left,bridge,right) at every node. Denote the resulting word
by Hsub_n, and its ACTUAL mass by Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n. Accepted
Sections 11--12 apply for every seed satisfying (1), including arbitrary
PSD remainders and zero steps. They give

    length(Hsub_n)=mn,
    W_(mn)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n,
    (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2<=Dsub_n<=A B_n.             (5)

The upper objective certificate holds for all functions in Section 1;
its matching Huber example gives the displayed equality. In particular
this is not merely a quadratic or projection-family assertion.

The length in (5) counts n old bridge steps and nr seed steps. The lower
mass bound pays for the one untouched dominant leaf: all primitive
capacities scale by A, while the square-root dominant merge is
1-Lipschitz in the square root of the right mass. This is why the
subtraction sqrt(A)-1 is present. We never physically replace the
dominant empty leaf by an object of mass A.

## 3. Integer-ray monotonicity and exact dyadic scaling

The admissible split k=0 in B_(n+1) gives

    B_(n+1)>=G(1,B_n)>B_n,

because B_n>0 and sqrt(B_n^2+8B_n)>B_n. Thus B is increasing directly
from the ORIGINAL recurrence. If positive integers b<=c, then

    B_(b*2^j-1)<=B_(c*2^j-1)

for every j. Taking the established limits proves Z_b<=Z_c. We do not
need or assert strict increase of the ray limits.

For every integer ell>=0, changing the limiting index gives

    Z_(2^ell b)
      =lim_j lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^(j+ell)-1)
      =lambda^ell Z_b.                                    (6)

This uses the accepted integer-ray limits only. No continuity, density,
or regularity of a real-valued phase function has been assumed.

## 4. The finite product and its strict margin

Choose positive integers b_pc0,d,K and an integer ell>=0. Let C_pc be a
positive certified upper bound for Z_d/Z_(b_pc0). Suppose

    m^K b_pc0<=2^ell d.

Define, for 0<=i<=K,

    b_pc_i=b_pc0 m^i,
    P_pc=lambda^ell C_pc.

All quantities Z_b are positive. Exact telescoping, monotonicity, and
(6), in that order, prove

    product_(i=0)^(K-1) [Z_(m b_pc_i)/Z_(b_pc_i)]
      =Z_(m^K b_pc0)/Z_(b_pc0)
      <=Z_(2^ell d)/Z_(b_pc0)
      =lambda^ell Z_d/Z_(b_pc0)
      <=P_pc.                                             (7)

Every inequality has the direction required for an UPPER product bound.
In particular this argument never turns an upper ray enclosure into a
lower bound for an unknown ratio.

Also m^K b_pc0>=b_pc0, so monotonicity makes the telescoped ratio at least 1.
Thus P_pc>=1, and the strict capacity condition A^K>P_pc implies A>1.

It is convenient to allow a certified capacity floor a with 1<a<=A.
Assume a^K>P_pc and choose a positive gamma satisfying

    K gamma<=1-P_pc/a^K.                                  (8)

Necessarily gamma<1. Write

    q_pc_i(a)=Z_(m b_pc_i)/(a Z_(b_pc_i)).

The product of the q_pc_i(a) is at most t_pc=P_pc/a^K<1. Therefore some
index i_star in {0,...,K-1} satisfies q_pc_i_star(a)<=t_pc^(1/K).
Otherwise their product would exceed t_pc.

For completeness, for x in [0,1] the inequality
(1-x)^K>=1-Kx follows by induction: multiplying the induction bound by
1-x adds the nonnegative term K x^2. With x=gamma, (8) gives

    (1-gamma)^K>=1-K gamma>=t_pc.

Consequently there is a fixed index i_star with

    Z_(m b_pc_i_star)/(A Z_(b_pc_i_star))
      <=q_pc_i_star(a)<=1-gamma.                             (9)

This is an existence assertion about a finite list, not a selection of
an unproved optimal phase. Sections 5--6 make it a physical and certified
statement.

## 5. Physical families, exact offsets, and uniform limit passage

For each i in {0,...,K-1} and integer j>=0, put

    n_(i,j)=b_pc_i 2^j-1,
    N_(i,j)=m n_(i,j)=m b_pc_i 2^j-m,
    H_(i,j)=Hsub_(n_(i,j)).

Thus there are K explicitly defined schedule families. At a fixed j
there are exactly K specified finite physical words, possibly with
different lengths. Each word is generated by the procedure in Section 2,
for example with the least maximizing split used to resolve ties.

The numerator is evaluated at its TRUE physical horizon. Equation (3)
applies with ray m b_pc_i and shift d=m-1=r, since

    N_(i,j)=m b_pc_i 2^j-1-(m-1).

It proves lambda^(-j)B_(N_(i,j))->Z_(m b_pc_i). Also (5) yields

    0<=A Z_(b_pc_i)-lambda^(-j)Dsub_(n_(i,j))
      <=A E(b_pc_i,j)
        +2(sqrt(A)-1)sqrt(A Z_(b_pc_i))lambda^(-j/2).           (10)

To see the second term, subtract the square in (5) from A B_n,
discard the negative term -(sqrt(A)-1)^2, and use
B_(n_(i,j))<=lambda^j Z_(b_pc_i). Thus the denominator converges to
A Z_(b_pc_i)>0. By (5),

    lim_j B_(N_(i,j)) W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
        =Z_(m b_pc_i)/(A Z_(b_pc_i)).                             (11)

There are only K indices. In (3) use the maximum ray m b_pc_(K-1) and
the fixed shift m-1; in (10) use b_pc_(K-1) and Z_(b_pc_(K-1)). These give
common errors tending to zero, while every denominator limit is at least
A because Z_b>=L_pc(b,0)=B_(b-1)>=1. Therefore (11) is uniform over
the finite list and over all maximizing-tree ties. No interchange of
an infinite phase minimum with a limit is involved.

For any one fixed selected i, the integers N_(i,j) strictly increase
and tend to infinity with j. The words at different horizons need not
be prefixes of one another; the original prescribed-horizon guarantee
does not require that property.

## 6. Certified phase selection and an explicit common horizon

At a search depth t>=0 compute, or supply certified enclosures for,
the K quantities

    F_pc_i(t)=U_pc(m b_pc_i,t)/(a L_pc(b_pc_i,t)).                   (12)

They are valid upper bounds on q_pc_i(a): the numerator is bounded from
above and the positive denominator from below. Both endpoints in (2)
converge, so F_pc_i(t)->q_pc_i(a). Choose the first t for which some

    F_pc_i(t)<1-gamma/2,                                    (13)

and freeze the smallest such i. This rule terminates: the index supplied
by (9) has limit at most 1-gamma, leaving the strict slack gamma/2.
It is unnecessary to decide which index minimizes the true ray ratio.

This is a certified convergent procedure, not a prescription to use
uncertified floating-point comparisons. For rational a and gamma, it has
the following finite-precision implementation in principle. At stage
t>=1, form outward rational enclosures of each L_pc(b_pc_i,t) and
U_pc(m b_pc_i,t) with absolute error at most 2^(-t), keeping every lower
denominator positive. Use the lower L endpoint and upper U endpoint
in (12), and stop only upon the strict rational inequality (13).
Each finite B value is defined by finitely many additions, products,
positive square roots, and maxima in the ORIGINAL recurrence. These
operations admit arbitrarily accurate rational outward enclosures:
rational square comparisons bracket each positive square root, and the
maximum of lower or upper endpoints bounds the corresponding maximum.
For a fixed stage only finitely many such operations are needed. The
recurrences have positive inputs, so arbitrary prescribed absolute
accuracy follows by refinement. The extra errors tend to zero with t,
and termination follows from the same strict slack. There is no claim
that this procedure is computationally economical, and it was not run.

More generally, an arbitrary real seed need not come with a computable
capacity oracle. The effective input is a certified rational floor
1<a<=A with a^K>P_pc, and a certified positive rational gamma satisfying
(8). If A^K>P_pc, such choices exist by strictness and rational density;
their certification uses the supplied seed data. This states the actual
input required for effective selection rather than presuming an oracle
for arbitrary real numbers. The exact-real formulation (12)--(13) is
also a valid mathematical selection rule.

It remains to turn the selected ratio into a bound for every sufficiently
large physical horizon. Put b_pc_max=b_pc_(K-1) and

    J=ceil(log(60 b_pc_max/gamma)/log(3/2)).                  (14)

This can equivalently be selected by the finite integer test
(3/2)^J>=60 b_pc_max/gamma, with rational arithmetic when gamma is rational.
Its finiteness follows from 3/2>1.

Here is a common error bound with no unproved rates. Since
sqrt(2)>5/4, lambda>9/4 and sqrt(lambda)>3/2. Also sqrt(2)<3/2,
and (4) gives rho-1<1 and rho/2<1. For b>=1,
j>=0, and q=(2/3)^j,

    E(b,j)+2lambda^(-j/2)
       <[12b+(3/25)(b+1)+9/10000+2]q
       <=[12+6/25+9/10000+2]b q
       <15b q.                                          (15)

Every inequality uses nonnegative terms; 2^(-j)<=q and
lambda^(-j)<=q as well. Thus for all i and all j>=J,

    E(b_pc_i,j)+2u_j<gamma/4,   u_j=lambda^(-j/2).            (16)

By (2) and Z_(b_pc_i)>=1,

    B_(n_(i,j))>=lambda^j Z_(b_pc_i)(1-E(b_pc_i,j)).

Also (4) implies B_(n_(i,j))>=lambda^j, so

    (1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_(n_(i,j)))<=u_j.

Using (5), monotonicity, and the LOWER ray enclosure for the larger ray,

    B_(N_(i,j))<=B_(m b_pc_i 2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_(m b_pc_i),

we get, whenever (16) holds,

    B_(N_(i,j))W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
       <=[Z_(m b_pc_i)/(A Z_(b_pc_i))]
          /[(1-E(b_pc_i,j))(1-u_j)^2].                      (17)

The factors in the denominator are positive by (16). For 0<=E,u<1,

    (1-E)(1-u)^2
       =1-E-2u+2Eu+(1-E)u^2>=1-E-2u.

Hence that denominator is greater than 1-gamma/4. For the fixed index
selected by (13), Z_(m b_pc_i)/(A Z_(b_pc_i))<=q_pc_i(a)<1-gamma/2, so

    B_(N_(i,j))W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))
       <(1-gamma/2)/(1-gamma/4)
       <1-gamma/4,            EVERY j>=J.                (18)

The last strict inequality follows from
(1-gamma/4)^2-(1-gamma/2)=gamma^2/16>0. Thus the explicit positive
relative separation is gamma/4, and the chosen phase is fixed before
the unbounded horizon sequence is used. The family and selection depend
only on the seed and benchmark, never on an objective or its trajectory.

## 7. The exponent interface follows from a finite integer cover

Take b_pc0=d=1 and C_pc=1. Then (7) becomes precisely

    product_(i=0)^(K-1) Z_(m^(i+1))/Z_(m^i)<=lambda^ell
       whenever m^K<=2^ell.                              (19)

Thus A^K>lambda^ell is sufficient. This finite condition for some
integers K>=1,ell>=0 is equivalent to A>m^rho. In one direction,
lambda^ell=(2^ell)^rho>=m^(K rho), so the strict power inequality implies
A>m^rho. Conversely, if A>m^rho, choose an integer K with

    K log(A/m^rho)>log(lambda)

and set ell=ceil(K log_2(m)). Then m^K<=2^ell<2m^K, and

    lambda^ell<lambda m^(K rho)<A^K.                     (20)

Here rho>0 and lambda=2^rho justify raising inequalities to rho.
If desired first choose a rational a with m^rho<a<A and perform the
same argument with a; Sections 4--6 then apply with certified inputs.
This proves sufficiency of A>(r+1)^rho without a claim about an
exponent obstruction or capacity ceiling for arbitrary seeds.

For an accepted upper comparison Z_d/Z_(b_pc0)<=C_pc, the stronger finite
interface available from (7) is

    A>(lambda^ell C_pc)^(1/K),
    m^K b_pc0<=2^ell d.                                    (21)

The word "stronger" refers to using this additional certified comparison;
it is not an assertion that every choice in (21) beats (19), or that
its value is below m^rho. For instance, at K=1,ell=0, b_pc0=3 and d=3m,
the accepted constants in `4c92d3fc4eb6153e` recover its sufficient
capacity hypotheses for r=5 and r=6. Its more specialized horizon and
margin estimates remain valid in their stated scope.

## 8. Exact rational examples, with no evaluator

All checks here are written as explicit integer equalities or positive
integer differences. They are mathematical parts of the joint proof,
not reports of computations executed by a program.

First record small power identities:

    lambda^4=17+12sqrt(2),
    lambda^5=41+29sqrt(2),
    lambda^10=3363+2378sqrt(2).

The last conjugate is lambda^(-10)>0, so

    lambda^10<lambda^10+lambda^(-10)=6726<6750.            (22)

Also sqrt(2)<17/12 and sqrt(2)<99/70, because respectively
289>288 and 9801>9800. Hence

    lambda<29/12,  lambda<169/70,  lambda^4<34.            (23)

### 8.1 Length five: capacity 49/5 and seventeen phases

Let m=6, a=49/5, K=17, ell=44, b_pc0=d=1, C_pc=1. The length cover is

    6^17<=2^44
      iff 3^17<=2^27,
    3^17=129140163<134217728=2^27.                        (24)

The capacity power bound follows from small factored checks. First

    49^4-9220*5^4=5764801-5762500=2301>0,

so a^4>9220. Next

    922^2=850084,  675^2=455625,
    200*850084-373*455625
      =170016800-169948125=68675>0,
    49*373^2-170*200^2
      =6817321-6800000=17321>0.                          (25)

Consequently

    lambda^44/a^17
       <[34*6750^4]/[(49/5)*9220^4]
       =170*675^4/(49*922^4)
       <6800000/6817321<1.                              (26)

This proves the needed exact finite power inequality; no decimal
approximation to rho is used. More quantitatively,

    17321*10000=173210000>115894457=17*6817321,

so (8) holds with gamma=1/10000, even at the capacity floor a=49/5.
Every actual full seed with r=5 and A>=49/5 therefore has a phase in

    b_pc_i=6^i,             0<=i<=16,
    N_(i,j)=6^(i+1)2^j-6,

which the certified procedure selects. With

    J5=ceil(log(600000*6^16)/log(3/2)),

its physical words satisfy, for EVERY j>=J5,

    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/40000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.     (27)

The capacity 49/5 is below 99/10 by exactly 1/10.

### 8.2 Length six: capacity 12 and eleven phases

Let m=7, a=12, K=11, ell=31, b_pc0=d=1, C_pc=1. The exact length cover is

    7^11=1977326743<2147483648=2^31.                     (28)

Use (22)--(23) and the equality 6750=(125/32)12^3. Then

    lambda^31/12^11
       <6750^3*(169/70)/12^11
       =125^3*169/(32^3*70*12^2)
       =330078125/330301440<1,                          (29)

since the denominator minus the numerator is 223315. Furthermore

    223315*20000=4466300000>3633315840=11*330301440.

Thus (8) holds with gamma=1/20000. Every actual full seed with r=6
and A>=12 has a certified selectable phase in

    b_pc_i=7^i,             0<=i<=10,
    N_(i,j)=7^(i+1)2^j-7.

With J6=ceil(log(1200000*7^10)/log(3/2)), for EVERY j>=J6 it gives

    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-1/80000) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.     (30)

The capacity 12 is below 1201/100 by exactly 1/100.

### 8.3 An accepted comparison shortens the length-five list

For an additional finite illustration, take m=6, a=197/20, K=9,
ell=21, b_pc0=3, d=15, and C_pc=19299/2500. The accepted comparison in
`04219554e41d9750` is Z_15/Z_3<C_pc. The cover reduces to

    6^9*3<=2^21*15
      iff 3^9<=5*2^12,
    19683<20480.                                        (31)

The integer checks

    197^3-950*20^3=7645373-7600000=45373>0,
    4*19299=77196<77500=31*2500

give a^3>950 and C_pc<31/4. With (22)--(23),

    lambda^21*C_pc/a^9
      <[6750^2*(29/12)*(31/4)]/950^3
       =899*135^2/(48*50*19^3)
       =16384275/16461600
       =218457/219488<1.                               (32)

Here 135^2=18225, 19^3=6859, and
16461600-16384275=77325. Moreover

    1031*2000=2062000>1975392=9*219488.

Thus gamma=1/2000 satisfies (8). There are now only nine phases,

    b_pc_i=3*6^i,          0<=i<=8,
    N_(i,j)=18*6^i*2^j-6.

With J5a=ceil(log(360000*6^8)/log(3/2)), the selected phase gives
for EVERY j>=J5a the relative separation 1/8000. The required capacity
197/20 is below 99/10 by 1/20, but is above the smaller 49/5 capacity
in Section 8.1. These are different finite tradeoffs, not an ordering
of their unknown best true phases.

For this SAME K=9, the least unanchored dyadic exponent is 24, since

    2^23=8388608<10077696=6^9<16777216=2^24.

The product bound lambda^21*C_pc is strictly smaller than lambda^24:
C_pc<31/4<14<lambda^3, where lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)>14 follows from
sqrt(2)>7/5. This is a proved improvement over the unanchored finite
bound at this fixed K. It asserts no optimality over all finite covers.

## 9. Complete conditional theorem proved by the joint argument

Fix the original function class and ALL-split benchmark from Section 1.
Suppose an actual length-r seed, r>=1, satisfies EVERY condition (1),
with h>=0 and no coordinate identifications, and put m=r+1, A=1+sum h.
Let b_pc0,d,K be positive integers and ell>=0 an integer. Let C_pc>0 be
a proved upper bound for Z_d/Z_(b_pc0) and assume

    m^K b_pc0<=2^ell d,    A^K>lambda^ell C_pc.

Choose any certified floor 1<a<=A and gamma>0 satisfying

    a^K>lambda^ell C_pc,
    K gamma<=1-lambda^ell C_pc/a^K.

Such real choices exist, and rational choices exist under the same strict
inequality, as explained in Section 6. Define the K physical families
H_(i,j), the exact horizons N_(i,j)=m b_pc0 m^i 2^j-m, and J by (14).
Then the certified convergent rule (12)--(13), or its rational outward
enclosure version, selects a FIXED i in {0,...,K-1}, terminates after
finitely many stages, and for every integer j>=J gives

    W_(N_(i,j))(H_(i,j))<(1-gamma/4) U_(N_(i,j))^ZJ.

The horizons form an unbounded increasing integer sequence. The full
all-function upper certificates and Huber equalities are supplied by the
actual basic-tree construction (5); every limiting comparison and finite
bound uses these actual horizons. The family is finite in its phase
index, and the error estimates are uniform over that finite index and
all maximizing-tree ties.

In particular A>(r+1)^rho is a sufficient interface, witnessed by some
finite K and ell. The exact separate sufficient capacities A>=49/5 for
r=5 and A>=12 for r=6, with the phase lists, J values, and relative
margins (27) and (30), follow. A>=197/20 for r=5 has the shorter nine-phase
alternative in Section 8.3. None of these hypotheses supplies an actual
seed or its full multiplier array.

## 10. Scope of the conditional conclusion

The selected index is fixed before using the unbounded physical horizon
sequence. The construction proves prescribed-horizon full-function objective
guarantees. It makes no all-horizon or anytime claim and supplies no actual
seed satisfying the hypotheses. It asserts neither arbitrary-template
inflation nor any exponent-based finite capacity ceiling. The power A^K
arises only from a finite product of ray ratios; it is not the capacity
of a constructed seed. No real-base interpolation, phase optimality, or
unrestricted exact-profile resolution is asserted.

## intuition
A finite telescoping product of integer-ray ratios forces a good phase. Certified ray enclosures freeze one phase, and the accepted physical seed substitution turns its strict ratio margin into full-class objective guarantees at the true physical horizons.
